\displaystyle=\trace\bigl[(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}})({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}})^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{a}_{\hat{S}})\bigr].

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{F}

and
using
\|\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{F}\leq\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{F}
twice,
we find

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}})\|_{F}\|({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}})^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{a}_{\hat{S}})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}\|_{F}\|({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}})^{\dagger}\|_{op}\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{a}_{\hat{S}})\|_{F}

and the second factor equals

\|\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}\|_{F}=\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}\|_{F}
by (
1.15
) for the Frobenius norm.

We introduce the notation
\lesssim
to denote an inequality up to a constant that depends
on
\eta_{1},...,\eta_{4}
and
\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}),\phi_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})
only.
On
\Omega_{*}
we have the operator norm bound (
C.11
),
the bound
\|\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}\|_{F}\leq|\hat{S}|^{1/2}\|\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}\|_{op}\lesssim(|\hat{S}|n)^{1/2}
as well as

\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{a}_{\hat{S}})\|_{F}=\sqrt{T}\|\boldsymbol{a}_{\hat{S}}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{T}
so that

\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}\|_{op}\sqrt{ns}n^{-1}\sqrt{T}

and
\|\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}\|_{op}\leq\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{op}+\|\boldsymbol{X}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})\|_{F}\leq\sigma(\sqrt{T}+2\sqrt{n})+\sqrt{n}\bar{R}

thanks to
\Omega_{4}
and
Lemma

C.2
.
Since
T\leq n
and
\bar{R}\lesssim 1
under
1.1
, we have proved that

\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{sT}
holds on
\Omega_{*}

which is exactly the desired bound (
C.4
).

∎

See
C.9

Proof of
Lemma

C.9
.

Recall that we assume the normalization
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{a}\|^{2}=1
.
Following the notation in
[
5
]
we define the quantities:

\boldsymbol{u}_{0}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{a},\quad\boldsymbol{z}_{0}=\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\quad\boldsymbol{Q}_{0}=\boldsymbol{I}_{p\times p}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}.

We have the decomposition
\boldsymbol{X}=\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{Q}_{0}+\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}
,
the vector
\boldsymbol{z}_{0}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{Q}_{0}
and
\boldsymbol{z}_{0}
has distribution
\mathcal{N}_{n}(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{I}_{n\times n})
.
Given a value of
(\boldsymbol{E},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{Q}_{0})
, define the open set

U_{0}=\{\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}:(\boldsymbol{E},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{Q}_{0}+\boldsymbol{z}_{0}{\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}})\in\Omega_{*}\}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

Since
\Omega_{*}
is open , so is the set
U_{0}
.
Given a value of
(\boldsymbol{E},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{Q}_{0})
we also define the function

U_{0}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}
given by

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})={\textstyle\mathop{\rm arg\,min}_{\boldsymbol{B}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}}}\Big(\tfrac{1}{2nT}\|\boldsymbol{E}+(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{Q}_{0}+\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-\boldsymbol{B})\|_{F}^{2}+\lambda\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{2,1}\Big)

as well as

\boldsymbol{F}:U_{0}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T},\qquad\boldsymbol{F}:\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\mapsto(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{Q}_{0}+\boldsymbol{z}_{0}{\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}})({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0}){-\boldsymbol{B}^{*}}).

Since
\bar{R}\to 0
under
1.1
and
\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{op}n^{-1/2}

is bounded by an absolute constant on
\Omega_{4}
when
T\leq n
,

Lemma

C.6
shows that
\boldsymbol{F}
is
L
-Lipschitz
for some constant
L
of the form
L=\sigma(\eta_{1},...,\eta_{4},\boldsymbol{\Sigma})

where the constant depends only on
\eta_{1},...,\eta_{4}
and the minimal and
maximal eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
.
By Kirszbraun’s Theorem, there exists an
L
-Lipschitz function

\tilde{\boldsymbol{F}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}

which is an extension of
\boldsymbol{F}
, i.e., it satisfies
\boldsymbol{F}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})=\tilde{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})
for all
\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\in U_{0}
.
Since
\boldsymbol{F}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})
is bounded from above by
n^{1/2}{(1-\eta_{3})}\bar{R}
in
U_{0}
by

Lemma

C.2
, we define the function
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
by

\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})=\boldsymbol{\Pi}\circ\tilde{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})

where
\boldsymbol{\Pi}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
is the convex projection onto the Frobenius ball of radius

n^{1/2}\bar{R}
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
. Since convex projections are 1-Lipschitz functions,
the function
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}
is also an
L
-Lipschitz extension of
\boldsymbol{F}
.

If
\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{b})
denotes the Jacobian such that

{\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{b}-\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\mathbf{0})\boldsymbol{b}=\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{b})\boldsymbol{w}+o(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|)}
for all
\boldsymbol{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{T}
,
then
\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{b})=\mathsf{D}(\boldsymbol{b})
on
U_{0}

because two functions that coincide on an open set have the same gradient
on this open set.
This implies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\trace[\mathsf{D}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})]\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Big[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\trace[\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})]\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\trace[\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})]\Big)^{2}\Big]

where the second display simply follows from
I\{\Omega_{*}\}\leq 1
.
By the main result of
[
4
]

we find

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[(\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\trace[\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})])^{2}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\trace(\{\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\right]

for each
t=1,...,T
since
\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}_{n}({\mathbf{0}},\boldsymbol{I}_{n\times n})
.
Summing this inequality over
t=1,...,T
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\trace[\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})]\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\|\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{0})\|_{F}^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{R}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{R}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\Big]+\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I\{\Omega_{*}^{c}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\Big].

Note that the first term,
\bar{R}^{2}/\sigma^{2}
, converges to 0,
as stated in
Lemma

C.2
.
We now bound the third term, on
\Omega_{*}^{c}
.
The quantity
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}
is exactly the squared Frobenius norm of the
Jacobian of the map

\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
(this Jacobian has dimensions
(nT)\times n

but we do not need to write it explicitly or choose a specific vectorization
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
into
{\mathbb{R}}^{nT}
).
Since
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}
is
L
-Lipschitz,
the operator norm of the Jacobian is at most
L
.
Since the rank of the Jacobian of a map from
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
to any other linear
space is at most
n
,
the rank of the Jacobian is at most
n
.
If follows from
\|\boldsymbol{J}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\rank(\boldsymbol{J})\|\boldsymbol{J}\|_{op}^{2}
with
\boldsymbol{J}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(nT)\times n}
the Jacobian
of
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}
that

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{J}\|_{F}^{2}\leq nL^{2}

so that

\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}[I\{\Omega_{*}^{c}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}]\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})L^{2}/\sigma^{2}
which converges to 0 under
1.1
thanks to
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*})\to 1
in

Lemma

C.1
.

It remains to show that

\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}]
converges to 0. This quantity is equal to

\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}]
since the derivatives of
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}
and
\boldsymbol{F}
coincide on
U_{0}
.
To bound this quantity, we use the explicit formulae obtained in
Lemma

C.7

with

\|{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2\|{\mathsf{D}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}+2\|{\mathsf{D}^{**}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}
.
We can use the following property of Kronecker products.
If
\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{Q}
are two matrices, and
\boldsymbol{e}_{t}
is the
t
-th canonical basis
vector in
{\mathbb{R}}^{T}
, then by the mixed product property (
1.13
)

(C.12)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\trace[\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\otimes\boldsymbol{M}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\boldsymbol{Q}]

(C.13)

\displaystyle=\trace[\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big[(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\otimes\boldsymbol{M}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\Big]\boldsymbol{Q}]

(C.14)

\displaystyle=\trace[\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M})\Big]\boldsymbol{Q}]

(C.15)

\displaystyle=\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{M})\boldsymbol{Q}\|_{F}^{2}.

Since
\|{\mathsf{D}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2(\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\boldsymbol{e}_{t})^{2}\|\boldsymbol{I}_{n\times n}\|_{F}^{2}+2\|(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{{\hat{S}}})\big({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}{}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}}\big)^{\dagger}\big((({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a})\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}^{\top}\big)\|_{F}^{2}
,
thanks to (
C.15
)
with
\boldsymbol{M}=\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}
and
\boldsymbol{Q}=\big({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}{}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}}\big)^{\dagger}\big((({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a})\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}^{\top}\big)
for the second term we find

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathsf{D}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2n\|({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{{\hat{S}}})\big({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}{}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}}\big)^{\dagger}\big((({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a})\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}^{\top}\big)\|_{F}^{2}.

The first summand is bounded by

2n\|({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{F}^{2}\;\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2n\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1/2}\bar{R}\;\phi_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})

and the second summand by

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}2\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{{\hat{S}}})\|_{op}^{2}\;\|\big({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}{}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}}\big)^{\dagger}\|_{op}^{2}\;\|(({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a})\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}2\|\boldsymbol{X}_{{\hat{S}}}\|_{op}^{2}\;\|\big({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}{}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}}\big)^{\dagger}\|_{op}^{2}\;\|({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{F}^{2}\;\|\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phantom{\text{(iii)}}}}{{\leq}}2\|\boldsymbol{X}_{{\hat{S}}}\|_{op}^{2}\;\|\big({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}{}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}}\big)^{\dagger}\|_{op}^{2}\;\|({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{F}^{2}\;\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}\;\rank(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}})\|\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{S}}\|_{op}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\leq}}2(\phi_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})(1+\eta_{4})^{2}n)^{2}(\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-2}(1-\eta_{4})^{-4}n^{-2})(\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1/2}\bar{R}\;\phi_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}))\bar{s}

\displaystyle=2\phi_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{3}\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-5/2}\bar{s}\bar{R}.

Above,
(i) follows from
\|\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{U}\|_{F}\leq\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{N}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{F}
,
(ii) is a consequence of (
1.15
) and (iii) holds on
\Omega_{*}
.
Thus
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathsf{D}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim n\bar{R}
.

Likewise,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathsf{D}^{**}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{{\hat{S}}})\big({\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}{}^{\top}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{X}}}+nT{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}}\big)^{\dagger}((\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{a}_{\hat{S}})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\phi_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})(1+\eta_{4})^{2}n)(\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-2}(1-\eta_{4})^{-4}n^{-2})(8\sigma^{2}nT+2(1-\eta_{3})^{2}n\bar{R}^{2})\phi_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}T

Thus

\frac{1}{n\sigma^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\bar{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})\|_{F}^{2}]\lesssim\frac{\bar{R}}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{T}{n}
and the right hand side converges to
0
under
1.1
.
∎

D
Proof that
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*})\to 1

D.1
\Omega_{1}
: Restricted Eigenvalues for random matrices in multi-task learning

Proposition D.1
.

Let
\boldsymbol{G}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times p}
be a random matrix with i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries
and let
\mathcal{A}
be a subset of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}
with
\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}=1
for all

\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{A}
.

(i)

For any two
\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{A}
,

{\mathbb{P}}(|~\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}|\geq\sqrt{x}\|\boldsymbol{B}-\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F})\leq 6e^{{-x}}
for all
x>0
.

(ii)

\sup_{\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{A}}|~\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}|\leq{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{A}}|\trace[\boldsymbol{B}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}]|+\sqrt{x}

with probability at least
1-e^{-x}
,
where
\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries.

\sup_{\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathcal{A}}|~\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-\sqrt{n}|\leq{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{A}}|\trace[\boldsymbol{B}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}]|+\sqrt{x}

also holds with probability at least
1-{3}e^{-x}
.

(iii)

If
\boldsymbol{X}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}_{p}({\mathbf{0}},\boldsymbol{\Sigma})
rows with
\max_{j\in[p]}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{jj}\leq 1

and

(D.1)

\mathcal{C}=\{\boldsymbol{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}:\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{2,1}\leq\sqrt{k}\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\},

then with probability at least
1-{3}e^{-x}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathcal{C}:\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}=1}\Big|n^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-1\Big|=\sup_{\boldsymbol{B}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}:\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{C},\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}=1}\Big|n^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}-1\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{x/n}+n^{-1/2}{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{B}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}:\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{C},\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}=1}|\trace[\boldsymbol{B}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}]|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{x/n}+\sqrt{{[kT+k\log(p/k)}]/({\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})n})}

This implies that for any constant
\eta_{3}\in(0,1)
,
if
\{kT+k\log(p/k)\}/(n\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}))\to 0

then

{\mathbb{P}}(\max_{\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathcal{C}:\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}=1}\big|n^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-1|\leq\eta_{3})\to 1
.

The proof follows the argument from
[
32
]
, adapted to the multi-task setting.

Proof of (i).

We distinguish two cases.

Case (a):
\sqrt{xn}>n/4
.
In this case we use that

\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}\leq\|\boldsymbol{G}(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B})\|_{F}=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B})^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

and we apply
[
53
, Theorem 6.3.2]

to the vector
\vec(\boldsymbol{G}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{np\times 1}
and
the block diagonal matrix with
n
blocks, each block being
(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B})^{\top}
.
This yields

{\mathbb{P}}(|~\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}|\geq\sqrt{x}\|\boldsymbol{B}-\boldsymbol{A}\|_{op}+\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{B}-\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F})\leq 2e^{-x}.

Here,
\sqrt{n}\leq 4\sqrt{x}

and we can bound from above the first
term to obtain the desired bound.

Case (b):
\sqrt{xn}\leq n/4
.
Write

\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}+\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}}
.
We will use repeatedly the following concentration bounds:
if
\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathcal{N}_{q}(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{I}_{q\times q})
and
\boldsymbol{M}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{q\times q}

is symmetric positive semi-definite, then

(D.2)

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{z}-\trace\boldsymbol{M}<2\sqrt{x}\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{F}\right)\leq e^{-x}.

This is a straightforward consequence
of
[
31
, Lemma 1]

after diagonalizing the symmetric positive semi-definite matrix
\boldsymbol{M}
.
Furthermore, for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{q\times q}
,

(D.3)

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}\boldsymbol{z}-\trace\boldsymbol{M}>2\sqrt{x}\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{F}+2x\|\boldsymbol{M}\|_{op}\right)\leq e^{-x}

see for instance

[
10
, Example 2.12]
or

[
2
, Lemma 3.1]
.

If
\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{\top},...,\boldsymbol{g}_{n}^{\top}
are the rows of
\boldsymbol{G}
then

\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}

is of the above form with
q=np
and
\boldsymbol{M}
is
block diagonal with
n
blocks equal to
\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times p}
.
Thus

\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}^{2}\geq n\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}^{2}-2\sqrt{xn}\|\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\|_{F}\geq n-2\sqrt{xn}
with probability at least
1-e^{-x}
by (
D.2
) and thanks to
\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}=1
.
The same holds for a lower bound
on
\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}^{2}
.
For the numerator, thanks to (
D.3
),
with probability at least
1-e^{-x}
:

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B})(\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{B})^{\top}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{xn}\|(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B})(\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{B})^{\top}\|_{F}+2x\|(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B})(\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{B})^{\top}\|_{op}.

By the union bound,
with probability at least
1-3e^{-x}
,

\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{xn}\|(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B})(\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{B})^{\top}\|_{F}+2x\|(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B})(\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{B})^{\top}\|_{op}}{2(n-2\sqrt{xn})_{+}^{1/2}}.

Since here
\sqrt{xn}\leq n/4
,
the denominator is at
least
2(n/2)^{1/2}

and using the submultiplicativity of the Frobenius norm
with
\|\boldsymbol{A}+\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}\leq 2
for the numerator we find

\frac{\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}-\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}}{\|\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}}\leq 2\frac{\sqrt{xn}+x}{(n/2)^{1/2}}\leq\sqrt{x}.

∎

Proof of (ii).

Since (i) proves that the process
Z_{\boldsymbol{A}}=\|\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}

has subgaussian increment with respect to the Frobenius norm,
(ii) follows by Talagrand Majorizing Measure theorem, for example as stated in
[
32
, Theorem 4.1]
.

The second statement follows by taking a fixed
\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{A}

and using
|\sqrt{n}-\|\boldsymbol{G}{\boldsymbol{B}}\|_{F}|\leq\sqrt{x}
with probability
at least
1-2e^{-x}

by
[
53
, Theorem 6.3.2]
applied to the block diagonal matrix with
n
blocks,
each block being
\boldsymbol{B}^{\top}
.
∎

Proof of (iii).

Recall that
\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries.
By application of (ii), it is sufficient to control the Gaussian
width

(D.4)

{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{B}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}:\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathcal{C},\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}=1}|\trace[\boldsymbol{B}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}]|={\mathbb{E}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathcal{C}:\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}=1}|\trace[\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}]|.

Let
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathcal{C}
and
let
\boldsymbol{g}_{1}^{\top},...,\boldsymbol{g}_{p}^{\top}
be the rows of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}
.
For any fixed
j\in[p]
, the random vector
\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{T\times 1}

has
\mathcal{N}_{T}(\mathbf{0}_{1\times T},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{jj}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})
distribution.
By the triangle inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have
for some
m,t>0

\displaystyle|\trace[\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{G}^{\prime}]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{p}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{{\top}}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\|_{2}=\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{2,1}(m+t)+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{{\top}}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}(\|\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\|_{2}-m-t)

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\sqrt{k}(m+t)+\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\|\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\|_{2}-m-t)_{+}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

where for the second line we used that
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathcal{C}
.
We have
\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\|_{op}

if
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}=1
.
Next, we now define
m
such that

m^{2}
is the median of the
\chi^{2}_{T}
distribution,
and
t=\sqrt{2\log(p/k)}
.
As explained in the proof of
Proposition

D.2
around (
D.6
)
we have
m\leq\sqrt{T}

[
39
]

as well as

{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\|\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\|_{2}-m-t)_{+}^{2}\leq k
.
By the inequality
\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}\leq\sqrt{2(a+b)}
,
(
D.4
)
is bounded from above
by
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\|_{op}(\sqrt{{2}k(T+2\log(p/k))}+\sqrt{k})\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\sqrt{8k(T+\log(p/k))}

and the proof is complete.

∎

D.2
\Omega_{2}
: Control of the noise

Proposition D.2
.

Let
a_{+}=\max(0,a)
.
If
\boldsymbol{E}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
entries
and
\boldsymbol{X}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times p}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}_{p}({\mathbf{0}},\boldsymbol{\Sigma})
rows independent of
\boldsymbol{E}
,
then

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Big(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}}{\sigma(1+\eta_{1})\sqrt{n\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{jj}}}-\sqrt{T}-\sqrt{2\log(p/s)}\Big)_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Big(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}}{\sigma\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}}-\sqrt{T}-\sqrt{2\log(p/s)}\Big)^{2}

(D.5)

\displaystyle\leq s

with probability at least
1-{4}/\{(2\log(p/s)+2)(4\pi\log(p/s)+4)^{1/2}\}-pe^{-n\eta_{1}^{2}/2}
.
Consequently, on the same event with

\lambda_{0}=\Big(\max_{j=1,...,p}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{jj}^{1/2}\Big)\frac{\sigma(1+\eta_{1})}{\sqrt{nT}}\Big(1+\sqrt{(2/T)\log(p/s)}\Big)

we have

\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}-nT\lambda_{0})_{+}^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}(1+\eta_{1})^{2}n\max_{j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{jj}s\leq sn^{2}T\lambda_{0}^{2}
.

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{jj})
entries,

{\mathbb{P}}(\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{jj}^{1/2}(\sqrt{n}+t))\leq e^{-t^{2}/2}

holds by standard bounds on
\chi^{2}_{n}
random variables, e.g.,
as a consequence of
[
10
, Theorem 5.5]
.
The choice
t=\eta_{1}\sqrt{n}
and
the union bound over
\{1,...,p\}
provides the first inequality in
(
D.5
).

Since
\boldsymbol{E}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{X}
,
conditionally on
\boldsymbol{X}
the random variable
\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}/(\sigma\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2})

has standard normal distribution
\mathcal{N}_{T}({\mathbf{0}},\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})
.
Since the conditional distribution does not depend on
\boldsymbol{X}
,
the unconditional distribution of
\boldsymbol{g}_{j}
is also
\mathcal{N}_{T}({\mathbf{0}},\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})
.
By
[
10
, Theorem 10.17]
applied
to the 1-Lipschitz function
\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\mapsto\|\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\|_{2}
,
inequality

{\mathbb{P}}(\|\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\|_{2}\geq m_{j}+t)\leq{\mathbb{P}}(Z_{j}\geq t)
holds,
where
Z_{j}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
m_{j}
is the median of the random variable

\|\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\|_{2}
. It follows that for any
t>0

(D.6)

W\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\|\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\|_{2}-m_{j}-t)_{+}^{2}\quad\text{ satisfies }\quad{\mathbb{E}}[W]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}(Z_{j}-t)_{+}^{2}\leq\frac{4pe^{-t^{2}/2}}{(t^{2}+2)(2\pi t^{2}+4)^{1/2}},

where the second inequality follows from
[
4
, Lemma G.1]
.
By the argument in
[
39
]
,
the median of the
\chi^{2}_{T}
distribution is smaller than
T

so that
m_{j}\leq\sqrt{T}
. Furthermore,
for
t=(2\log(p/s))^{1/2}
we have
{\mathbb{E}}[W]\leq sq

where
q^{-1}=(t^{2}+2)(2\pi t^{2}+4)^{1/2}/4>1
. The second inequality in
(
D.5
) thus holds with probability at least
1-q

by Markov’s inequality
{\mathbb{P}}(W>{\mathbb{E}}[W]q^{-1})\leq q
.
∎

D.3
\Omega_{3}
: Restricted Isometry Properties

The following bound is well known in the literature on the RIP property
for Gaussian matrices, as a consequence of Gordon’s Lemma, see,
e.g.,
[
55
]
.
We provide the argument here for completeness.

Proposition D.3

(Bound on upper sparse eigenvalues of random matrices, Gordon’s lemma)
.

Let
p\geq n
. If
\boldsymbol{X}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times p}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\boldsymbol{\Sigma})
rows, then

(i) for any set
B\subset[p]
we have

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\max_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}:\supp(\boldsymbol{v})\subset B}\Big|\frac{\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{v}\|}{\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{v}\|}-1\Big|\leq\sqrt{|B|/n}+t\Big)\geq 1-2e^{-nt^{2}/2}

by Gordon’s escape through the mesh theorem
and its consequence,
cf. for instance in
[
18
, Theorem II.13]

applied to the Gaussian matrix
\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}

and the intersection of the unit ball with the
|B|
dimensional
linear span of
\{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{e}_{j},j\in B\}
.

(ii) Let
\eta_{4}\in(0,1)
be a constant.
If
k
is such that
{\sqrt{k/n}}\leq\eta_{4}/2

and

k\log(ep/k)/n\leq\eta_{4}^{2}/16
, then
simultaneously for all
B
with
|B|\leq k

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{B\subset[p]:|B|\leq k}\Big(\max_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}:\supp(\boldsymbol{v})\subset B}\Big|\frac{\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{v}\|}{\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{v}\|}-1\Big|\Big)>\eta_{4}\right)\leq 2\exp(-n\eta_{4}^{2}/16).

Proof.

For (ii),
by the union bound with
t=\eta_{4}/2
we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{B\subset[p]:|B|\leq k}\Big(\max_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}:\supp(\boldsymbol{v})\subset B}\Big|\frac{\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{v}\|}{\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{v}\|}-1\Big|\Big)>\eta_{4}\right)\leq 2\binom{p}{{k}}e^{-n\eta_{4}^{2}/8}.

Since
\log\binom{p}{k}\leq k\log(e{p}/k)
, the right hand side
is bounded from above by
2\exp(-n\eta_{4}^{2}/16)
by assumption on
k
.
∎

E
Proof of Theorems
3.1
and
3.2

Proof.

By replacing
\boldsymbol{b}
by
\boldsymbol{b}/\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}
if necessary,
we assume that
\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}=1
without loss of generality.
The proof is based on the decomposition

\displaystyle{(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}}\big(n\boldsymbol{a}^{T}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\boldsymbol{b}+\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{T}(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\big)

\displaystyle={(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}}{\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{T}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{b}}+\boldsymbol{r}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}+{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}

with the remainder terms

\boldsymbol{r}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}
and
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}

defined by the random vectors
\boldsymbol{r},{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{T}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{r}^{\top}

\displaystyle=(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{T}\boldsymbol{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}-(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})\right],

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}^{\top}

\displaystyle={(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}}\left[\boldsymbol{a}^{T}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})-\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{T}\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\right](\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}.

Since
\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}_{n}({\mathbf{0}},\boldsymbol{I}_{n\times n})
is independent of
\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{b}\sim\mathcal{N}_{n}(\mathbf{0},\sigma^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{n\times n})
we have
\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{b}/\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
.
Since
\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}n^{-1}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1
by the law of large numbers,
we obtain that
(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{b}\smash{\xrightarrow{d}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

by Slutsky’s theorem.
To conclude with another application of Slutsky’s theorem,
it remains to prove that
\|\boldsymbol{r}\|_{2}
and
\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}\|_{2}
both converge to 0
in probability, and to prove
that for the denominator,
(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1
.

For
\boldsymbol{r}
,
on
\Omega_{*}
we have

\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}-(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})\|_{op}\leq\bar{s}/(n-\bar{s})

by
Proposition

2.1
(iii) and
Lemma

C.3
.
It follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\min(1,\|\boldsymbol{r}\|_{2})]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})+{\mathbb{E}}\big[I\{\Omega_{*}\}(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}-(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})\|_{op}\big]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})+(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\big(\bar{s}/(n-\bar{s})\big){\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})+(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\big(\bar{s}/(n-\bar{s})\big)\sqrt{nT\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})+\big(\bar{s}/(n-\bar{s})\big)\sqrt{T}

by Jensen’s inequality and
{\mathbb{E}}[\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}]=nT\sigma^{2}
.
The last line converges to 0 by
Lemma

C.1

and
1.1
.
Since
W_{n}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}0
if and only if
{\mathbb{E}}[\min(1,|W_{n}|)]\to 0
,
this proves the convergence
\|\boldsymbol{r}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}0
.

For
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}
, we use that

{\mathbb{E}}[\min(1,\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}\|_{2})]\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})+{\mathbb{E}}[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}\|_{2}]

with
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})\to 0
as above. For the second term,
on
\Omega_{*}
we have

\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\|_{op}\leq\|\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\|_{op}+\|\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\|_{op}\leq 1+\bar{s}/(n-\bar{s})=(1-\bar{s}/n)^{-1}
by
Proposition

2.1
and
Lemma

C.3
. It follows that

\displaystyle I\{\Omega_{*}\}\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq I\{\Omega_{*}\}\tfrac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}(1-\tfrac{\bar{s}}{n})^{-1}\|(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}-({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=I\{\Omega_{*}\}\tfrac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}(1-\tfrac{\bar{s}}{n})^{-1}\Big[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\trace[{\mathsf{D^{*}}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})]-\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\Big)^{2}\Big]^{1/2}.

where the equality is a consequence of
Lemma

C.8
.
Since
\mathsf{D^{*}}=\mathsf{D}-\mathsf{D^{**}}
,
and using the inequalities
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
and
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
,

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\trace[{\mathsf{D^{*}}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})]\Big)^{2}\Big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big[2{\mathbb{E}}\Big(I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\big[\boldsymbol{z}_{0}^{\top}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\trace[{\mathsf{D}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})]\big]^{2}\Big)+2{\mathbb{E}}\Big(I\{\Omega_{*}\}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\trace[{\mathsf{D}^{**}}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t})]^{2}\Big)\Big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq o((n\sigma^{2})^{1/2})+O\big(\sigma\min(T,(sT)^{1/2})\big)

by
Lemma

C.9
and
inequality (
C.4
) in
Lemma

C.8
.
Combining the above displays yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\min(1,\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}\|_{2})]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})+(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}(1-\tfrac{\bar{s}}{n})^{-1}\big[o((n\sigma^{2})^{1/2})+O\big(\sigma(sT)^{1/2}\big)\big]=o(1),

or equivalently
\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{r}}}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}0
.

Let us prove
Theorem

3.2
, that is

(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1
.
By the law of large numbers, we have

\|\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}^{2}/(n\sigma^{2})\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1
, so it suffices to show that

(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{E}\big[(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}-\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\big]\boldsymbol{b}-\boldsymbol{X}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}0.

Techniques similar to those above show that

(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{E}\big[(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}-\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\big]\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}0

by
Proposition

2.1
(iii),
and that

(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{X}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}0

by
Lemma

C.2
and
\bar{R}\to 0
.

An application of Slutsky’s lemma completes the proof of
Theorem

3.1
.
∎

F
Proof for
\chi^{2}_{T}
limits, and confidence ellipsoid with nominal coverage

Lemma F.1

(Differentiation with respect to
\boldsymbol{E}
)
.

Here, we consider differentiation with respect to
\boldsymbol{E}
for fixed
\boldsymbol{X}
.
We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})-\sigma^{2}\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{F}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}nT{\bar{R}}^{2}+\sigma^{4}nT.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{F}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
be the function

\boldsymbol{F}:\boldsymbol{E}\mapsto\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})
.
The function
\boldsymbol{F}
is 1-Lipschitz by
[
3
, Proposition 3.1]
.
Furthermore,
\|\boldsymbol{F}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{n}\bar{R}
on
\Omega_{*}
by
Lemma

C.2
,
so that if
\boldsymbol{\Pi}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
is the convex projection
onto the Frobenius ball of radius
\sqrt{n}\bar{R}
, the composition

\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}\circ\boldsymbol{F}
coincides with
\boldsymbol{F}
on
\Omega_{*}
.
The function
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}
is also 1-Lipschitz by composition of two
1-Lipschitz functions, and
since
\Omega_{*}
is open, the derivatives of
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}
and
\boldsymbol{F}

with respect to
\boldsymbol{E}
coincide in
\Omega_{*}
where the derivatives exist
(this existence of the derivatives is granted almost everywhere
by Rademacher’s theorem).

For any
t,t^{\prime}\in[T]
, by the main result of
[
4
]

applied to the function
\boldsymbol{E}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\mapsto\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}
,
we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\bigl(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\sigma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}\bigr)^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\sigma^{4}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial E_{i^{\prime}t^{\prime}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\Big)\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\sigma^{4}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial E_{i^{\prime}t^{\prime}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\Big)^{2}\Big].

We now sum the above inequalities for all
t,t^{\prime}\in[T]
to find

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\bigl(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\sigma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}\bigr)^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}T{\mathbb{E}}[\|\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\|_{F}^{2}]+\sigma^{4}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial E_{i^{\prime}t^{\prime}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}Tn\bar{R}^{2}+\sigma^{4}nT,

where for the last inequality we used that

\|\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}\|_{F}\leq\bar{R}\sqrt{n}
by construction of
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}

and that
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
is 1-Lipschitz, so that
the Frobenius norm of the Jacobian of
\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}
(which is a matrix of size
(nT)\times(nT)
) is at most
\sqrt{nT}
.
Finally, on
\Omega_{*}
we have
\boldsymbol{F}=\bar{\boldsymbol{F}}
and their derivatives
coincide, and by differentiating the KKT conditions of
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}
we find

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}=\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}_{tt^{\prime}}
on
\Omega_{*}
for
\boldsymbol{F}=\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Theorem F.2
.

Let
\boldsymbol{a}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
with
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}=1
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\xi}
be defined in (
4.1
)
and
\hat{\sigma}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}\|_{F}^{2}/(nT)
.
Then under
1.1
,

|\hat{\sigma}/\sigma-1|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(T^{-1/2})
as well as

(F.1)

\max\{(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2},(\hat{\sigma}^{2}n)^{-1/2}\}\big\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{-1}\big\|_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Proof of
Theorem

F.2
.

By definition of
\boldsymbol{\xi}
we have

(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}=(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\|({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}-(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}

which converges to 0 in probability by
Lemma

C.9
.
Next, with
\chi^{2}_{T}=\sigma^{-2}\big\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}
,

(F.2)

(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\big\|\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\big\|_{2}=(\chi_{T}^{2})^{1/2}\big|1-n^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}\big|.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
we have
{\mathbb{E}}[(\chi_{T}^{2})^{1/2}\big|1-n^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}\big|]\leq\sqrt{T/n}\,{\mathbb{E}}[(\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}-\sqrt{n})]^{1/2}
.
Combining Theorem 3.1.1 and Equation 2.15 in
[
53
]
yields

{\mathbb{E}}[(\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}-\sqrt{n})]^{1/2}\leq C
for some absolute constant
C
.
Thus, by
1.1
we have
T/n\to 0
so that
(
F.2
)
converges to
0
in
L^{1}
, hence in probability.
This proves
(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\big\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{-1}\big\|_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

We now prove the same bound with
\sigma^{2}n
replaced by
\hat{\sigma}^{2}n
.
Let
\Omega_{8}=\{|\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{F}/\sigma-\sqrt{nT}|\leq\sqrt{\log n}\}
.
Then
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{8})\to 1
by
[
53
, Theorem 3.1.1]

and

\displaystyle I\{\Omega_{8}\cap\Omega_{*}\}|\hat{\sigma}/\sigma-1|

\displaystyle\leq I\{\Omega_{*}\}\|\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{F}(\sigma^{2}nT)^{-1/2}+I\{\Omega_{8}\}|\sqrt{nT}-\|\boldsymbol{E}\|_{F}/\sigma|(nT)^{-1/2}

(F.3)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\eta_{3})\bar{R}/\sqrt{\sigma^{2}T}+(nT)^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log n}

by
Lemma

C.2
for the first term.
This proves that
|\hat{\sigma}/\sigma-1|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(T^{-1/2})
.
under
1.1
so that using

\frac{1}{2}|\frac{1}{u}-1|\leq|u-1|
for
u\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
we obtain for
n
large enough

(1/2)I\{\Omega_{8}\cap\Omega_{*}\}|\sigma/\hat{\sigma}-1|\leq I\{\Omega_{8}\cap\Omega_{*}\}|\hat{\sigma}/\sigma-1|\leq\eqref{upper-bound-sigma-hat-sigma}.

Hence
\sigma/{\hat{\sigma}}=1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, thus

\displaystyle(n\hat{\sigma}^{2})^{-1/2}\big\|\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=(\sigma/{\hat{\sigma}})(n\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}\big\|\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=(1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1))o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

∎

See
4.1

Proof of
Theorem

4.1
.

Theorem

F.2
applied with
\boldsymbol{z}=\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\|_{2}^{-1}

yields the bound

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
The proof then follows from
Lemma

F.3
.
∎

Lemma F.3
.

Let
1.1
be fulfilled.
Let
\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\xi}
be random vectors valued in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Assume that

\boldsymbol{z}
is a measurable function of
\boldsymbol{X}
with
{\mathbb{P}}(\|\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}=1)=1

and let
\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}=\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-\boldsymbol{z}\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}
.
Then the random variable
F_{T,n-T}=\frac{n-T}{T}\|{({\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E}})^{-1/2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}^{2}

has the
F
distribution with degrees of freedom

T
and
n-T
, and the following holds:

(i)

\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}=\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{T}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

as
n\to+\infty
when
T/n\to 0
where
\chi^{2}_{T}

is a random variable with chi-square distribution with
T

degrees of freedom,

(ii)

{\mathbb{P}}(\lambda_{\min}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\geq n\sigma^{2}/2)\to 1
,

(iii)

\sqrt{n-T}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}-\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}\leq o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{T}{\sqrt{n}})
,

(iv)

\sqrt{n-T}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}-\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}\geq-o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)-O_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{T}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{sT+s\log(p/s)}{n}\sqrt{T})
,

(v)

\sqrt{n-T}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}-\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}\leq o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{s(s+T)\log^{2}(p/s)}{n\sqrt{T}})
.

Consequently, if
(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
then

(F.4)

\displaystyle(1-\tfrac{T}{n})^{1/2}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)+O_{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\min\bigl\{\tfrac{T}{\sqrt{n}},\tfrac{\log^{2}(p/s)}{n^{1/4}}\bigr\}\bigr)

(F.5)

\displaystyle(1-\tfrac{T}{n})^{1/2}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq(\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}-o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)-O_{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\tfrac{T}{\sqrt{n}}+\tfrac{(sT+s\log\tfrac{p}{s})\sqrt{T}}{n}\bigr).

Proof of
Lemma

F.3
.

For (i), we introduce the quantity

(F.6)

H\coloneqq(n-1)\|({\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E}})^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}^{2}=(n-1)\boldsymbol{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{W}^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}

where
\boldsymbol{g}=\sigma^{-1}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}

and
\boldsymbol{W}=\sigma^{-2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E}
.
Since
\boldsymbol{E}
and
\boldsymbol{z}
are independent and since
\|\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}=1
,

\boldsymbol{g}
has distribution
\mathcal{N}_{T}(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})
.

\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}
can be orthogonally diagonalized as
\boldsymbol{Q}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}{}^{\top}\big)\boldsymbol{Q}^{\top}

where
\boldsymbol{Q}
is an
n\times n
orthogonal matrix, thus

\boldsymbol{W}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\boldsymbol{n}_{i}\boldsymbol{n}_{i}^{\top}

where the random vectors
\boldsymbol{n}_{i}=\sigma^{-1}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}

are iid with standard normal

\mathcal{N}_{T}(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})
distribution.
Therefore
\boldsymbol{W}
has the
Wishart distribution with identity covariance and
n-1
degrees-of-freedom.
Since
\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}
and
\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}
are independent, so are
\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}

and
(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp})(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp})^{\top}=\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E}
,
thus
\boldsymbol{g}
and
\boldsymbol{W}
are independent.
By
[
21
, Theorem 5.8]

H
has the Hotelling distribution with parameters
T,n-1
,
and

\frac{n-1-T+1}{T}\frac{H}{n-1}\sim F_{T,n-1-T+1}=F_{T,n-T}

where the right-hand side is the
F
distribution with
degrees-of-freedom
T
and
n-T
.
Furthermore, since
F_{T,n-T}=\frac{\chi^{2}_{T}/T}{\chi^{2}_{n-T}/(n-T)}

for some random variables having chi-square distributions with respective parameter
T
and
n-T
,
we have

|\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}-\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{T}}|=|\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{T}/(\chi^{2}_{n-T}/(n-T))}-\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{T}}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{T})|1-\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{n-T}/(n-T)}|

where the last equality follows from
{\mathbb{E}}[(\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}]\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\chi^{2}_{T}]^{1/2}=\sqrt{T}
and
the a.s. convergence of
\chi^{2}_{n-T}/(n-T)
to
1
.
Furthermore
|1-|a||\leq|1-a^{2}|
and the Central Limit Theorem yield

|1-\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{n-T}/(n-T)}|\leq\frac{|\chi^{2}_{n-T}-(n-T)|}{n-T}=O_{\mathbb{P}}((n-T)^{-1/2}).

Thus
\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}=(\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}+O_{\mathbb{P}}((\frac{n}{T}-1)^{-1/2})
,
and since
\frac{n}{T}\to 0

we have
\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}=(\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

and
{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}\leq q_{T,\alpha})\to 1-\alpha

by
Proposition

4.2
. This proves (i).

Next we exhibit a lower bound on the eigenvalues of
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{H}={\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*}
and consider the decomposition

(F.7)

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}=\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}+(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H})-[\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}+(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H})^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}].

Since
(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H})
is positive semidefinite we have

(F.8)

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}~\succeq~\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}-2\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}.

Since
\boldsymbol{E}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
entries,
if
s_{\min}(\boldsymbol{E})
and
s_{\max}(\boldsymbol{E})
denote the smallest and greatest singular values of
\boldsymbol{E}

we have
\sigma(\sqrt{n}-\sqrt{T})\leq{\mathbb{E}}[s_{\min}(\boldsymbol{E})]\leq{\mathbb{E}}[s_{\max}(\boldsymbol{E})]\leq\sigma(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{T})
by
[
18
, Theorem II.13]
.
Since
s_{\min}(\boldsymbol{E})
and
s_{\max}(\boldsymbol{E})
are
1
-Lipschitz functions of
\boldsymbol{E}
when considered as a vector in
\mathbb{R}^{nT}
,
Gaussian concentration as stated in
[
19
, Theorem B.6]
yields the existence of exponential random variables

Z_{1},Z_{2}\sim\text{Exp}(1)
such that almost surely

\sigma(\sqrt{n}-\sqrt{T}-\sqrt{2Z_{1}})\leq s_{\min}(\boldsymbol{E})\leq s_{\max}(\boldsymbol{E})\leq\sigma(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{2Z_{2}}).

Letting
Z=2\max(Z_{1},Z_{2})
, we have

\sigma^{2}(\sqrt{n}-\sqrt{T}-\sqrt{Z})_{+}^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}\preceq\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\preceq\sigma^{2}(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{Z})^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}.

Thanks to (
F.8
) and the inequality
(1-x)_{+}^{2}\geq 1-2x
for
x\geq 0

we have

(F.9)

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\succeq\sigma^{2}n[1-2(\sqrt{T/n}+\sqrt{Z/n})-2\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}/(\sigma^{2}n)]\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}.

On the event
\Omega_{9}=\{1-2(\sqrt{T/n}+\sqrt{Z/n})-2\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}/(\sigma^{2}n)>1/2\}
we have
\lambda_{\min}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}})\geq\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}-2\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})\geq\sigma^{2}n/2
.
We now proceed to show that
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{9})\to 1
.
We have by the triangle inequality for the norm
{\mathbb{E}}[(\cdot)^{2}]^{1/2}
that

(F.10)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\Big(\sqrt{T/n}+\sqrt{Z/n}+\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}/(\sigma^{2}n)\Big)^{2}\Big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T/n}+{\mathbb{E}}[Z]^{1/2}/\sqrt{n}+\bar{s}/n+{\mathbb{E}}[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}-\sigma^{2}\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{op}^{2}/(\sigma^{2}n)^{2}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T/n}+{\mathbb{E}}[Z]^{1/2}/\sqrt{n}+\bar{s}/n+[(T/n)(1+{\bar{R}}^{2}/\sigma^{2})]^{1/2}

where we used
Proposition

2.1
(ii) and
Lemma

C.3

to bound
\|\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{op}
from above by
\bar{s}
on
\Omega_{*}

for the first inequality, and

Lemma

F.1
the second inequality.
Hence under
1.1
, the previous display converges to 0.
Next,
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{9}^{c})={\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{9}^{c}\cap\Omega_{*}^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{9}^{c}\cap\Omega_{*})
,
Markov’s inequality and an application of Jensen’s
inequality yield

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{9}^{c})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c}\cap\Omega_{9}^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(1/4\leq I\{\Omega_{*}\}\big(\sqrt{T/n}+\sqrt{Z/n}+\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}/(\sigma^{2}n)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})+4{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I\{\Omega_{*}\}\big(\sqrt{T/n}+\sqrt{Z/n}+\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}/(\sigma^{2}n)\big)\Big]\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})+4\eqref{eq:upper-bound-TZnorm}

where
4\eqref{eq:upper-bound-TZnorm}
refers to four times the quantity
(
F.10
) which converges to 0.
Thus the event
\Omega_{9}
has probability
approaching one and claim (ii) follows.

We now prove (iii)-(v).
Let
\Omega(n)
be a sequence of events with
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega(n))\to 1
,

V_{n}
be any sequence of random variables and
a_{n}
be any deterministic sequence of real numbers.
It is easily seen that

I\{\Omega(n)\}V_{n}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(a_{n})
implies
V_{n}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(a_{n})

and
I\{\Omega(n)\}V_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(a_{n})
implies
V_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(a_{n})
.
This observation will allow us to transition seamlessly from bounds on
I\{\Omega(n)\}V_{n}
to bounds on
V_{n}

by choosing, e.g.,
\Omega(n)=\Omega_{*}\cap\Omega_{9}
or other events of
probability approaching one in our problem.
It will be useful to note that by the same argument as above

\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})\geq\sigma^{2}(\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-\sqrt{2Z_{3}})_{+}^{2}

where
Z_{3}\sim\text{Exp}(1)
,
so that
\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})\geq\sigma^{2}n/2

on an event
\Omega_{8}
of probability approaching one.
We will use the following fact: if
\boldsymbol{M},\boldsymbol{N}
are
two positive definite matrices with eigenvalues at least
1/2
then

(F.11)

\|\boldsymbol{M}^{-1/2}-\boldsymbol{N}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{M}^{1/2}-\boldsymbol{N}^{1/2}\|_{op}\leq\sqrt{2}\|\boldsymbol{M}-\boldsymbol{N}\|_{op}

using the resolvent identity

\boldsymbol{M}^{-1/2}-\boldsymbol{N}^{-1/2}=\boldsymbol{N}^{-1/2}(\boldsymbol{N}^{1/2}-\boldsymbol{M}^{1/2})\boldsymbol{M}^{-1/2}
for the first inequality
and
[
30
]
for the second.
To prove (iii), we apply (
F.11
)
to
\boldsymbol{M}=(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1}[\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}-2\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}]

and
\boldsymbol{N}=(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E}
, both matrices
having eigenvalues at least
1/2
on
\Omega_{9}\cap\Omega_{8}
.
Rewriting (
F.8
) as
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}{}^{-1/2}\preceq(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{M}^{-1/2}
,
applying the triangle inequality and (
F.11
), we have
on
\Omega_{8}\cap\Omega_{9}

(F.12)

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sqrt{n-T}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}{}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}-\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n-T}(\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}{}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}-\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{(n-T)/(\sigma^{2}n)}\|\boldsymbol{M}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}-\sqrt{(n-T)/(\sigma^{2}n)}\|\boldsymbol{N}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{(n-T)/(\sigma^{2}n)}\|(\boldsymbol{M}^{-1/2}-\boldsymbol{N}^{-1/2})\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1-T/n}\sqrt{2}\Big\|(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1}[\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}-2\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}]\Big\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}\sigma^{-1}.

The bounds used in (
F.10
) yield

I\{\Omega_{*}\}\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}-\sigma^{2}\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{op}(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{T/n})

and
I\{\Omega_{*}\}\|\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{s})
,
hence
\frac{\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}}{\sigma^{2}n}\leq\frac{\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}-\sigma^{2}\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{op}}{\sigma^{2}n}+\frac{\|\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{op}}{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{\sqrt{n}})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{\bar{s}}{n}).

Furthermore
\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}^{2}/\sigma^{2}
has
\chi^{2}_{T}
distribution, thus

\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}^{2}/\sigma^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(T)

and
we obtain

\Delta\leq\sqrt{1-T/n}\Big(O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{T}{n})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{\sqrt{T}}{\sqrt{n}})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{\bar{s}}{n})\Big)O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{T}).

Since
\frac{T}{n}\to 0
, the right-hand side of the equality is

O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{T}{\sqrt{n}})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{s\sqrt{T}}{n})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{T}{\sqrt{n}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

For claim (iv), with
\Delta
defined in (
F.12
) a similar argument yields

\displaystyle|\Delta|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n-T}\|\bigl({\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}{}^{-1/2}-(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1/2}\bigr)\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{1-T/n}(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1}\bigl[\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{op}^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}+\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}^{2}\bigr]\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}/\sigma

on
\Omega_{8}\cap\Omega_{9}
,
thus
|\Delta|\leq\sqrt{1-T/n}\bigl(O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{T}{n})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{\sqrt{T}}{\sqrt{n}})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tfrac{\bar{s}}{n})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{R}^{2})\bigr)O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{T})

thanks to
Lemma

C.2
(ii) for the term
\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{H}\|_{op}/(\sigma^{2}n)
.
This proves (iv).

It remains to prove (v), for which we need a more subtle argument.
The important remark is that on the one hand

\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\top}
is independent
of
\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}
because
\boldsymbol{E}
has iid
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
entries,
while on the other hand
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}
is not independent of
\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}
.
To overcome this lack of independence, we bound
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}
from below
by a positive definite matrix independent of
\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}
, as follows.
For a fixed subset
J\subset[p]
,
let
\boldsymbol{P}_{J}
be the orthogonal projection matrix
onto the linear span of

\{\boldsymbol{z}\}\cup\{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j},j\in J\}

so that the rank of
\boldsymbol{P}_{J}
is at most
|J|+1
.
Set
\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}=\boldsymbol{I}_{n\times n}-\boldsymbol{P}_{J}
. Then
in the event

(F.13)

\hat{S}\cup\supp(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\subset J,

we have
\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})=\mathbf{0}
, hence

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\succeq(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})=\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E}
,
thus

\displaystyle\sqrt{n-T}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n-T}\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}.

For a fixed
J
and in the event
\hat{S}\cup\supp\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\subset J
, we can bound from above
\Delta
in (
F.12
) as

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n-T}\bigl[\|({\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}-\|({\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{n-T}}{\|({\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}}\Bigl[\|({\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}^{2}-\|(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]_{+}

(F.14)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n-T}}{\|({\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}}\boldsymbol{E})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}}\Bigl[\boldsymbol{g}^{\top}\Bigl\{(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}-(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}\Bigr\}\boldsymbol{g}\Bigr]_{+}

where
\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\sim\mathcal{N}_{T}(\mathbf{0},\sigma^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})

as before,
the first inequality follows from

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}{}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\preceq(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and the second from

\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}\leq(a-b)_{+}/\sqrt{b}

.
For any
J\subset[p]
, the null space inclusion

\ker\boldsymbol{P}_{J}\subset\ker\boldsymbol{z}\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}
holds and
the matrix
\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}-\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}
is
an orthogonal projection matrix with rank
r\leq|J|
so that

\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}-\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}^{\top}

for the matrix
\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times{r}}
with orthonormal columns
given by
\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}
where

\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are orthonormal eigenvectors of
\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}-\boldsymbol{P}_{J}^{\perp}

corresponding to the non-zero eigenvalues and
\boldsymbol{e}_{k}
are
canonical basis vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
.
By the Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury identity, the matrix in curly brackets is equal to

\boldsymbol{M}_{J}\coloneqq(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{I}_{r\times r}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}\Bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}.

Applying
[
18
, Theorem II.13]
to the Gaussian
matrices
\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}
and
\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E}
, we find

(F.15)

\begin{split}{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}\|_{op}\geq\sigma(\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{|J|}+t)\bigr)&\leq e^{-t^{2}/2},\\
{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\phi_{\min}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})\leq\sigma^{2}(\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-t)_{+}^{2}\bigr)&\leq e^{-t^{2}/2}\end{split}

for all
t>0
. As long as
\frac{1}{2}\geq(\frac{\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{|J|}+t}{\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-t})^{2}
we have

\boldsymbol{I}_{r\times r}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}\succeq\boldsymbol{I}_{r\times r}/2

and thus
\boldsymbol{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{j}\boldsymbol{g}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}\|_{2}^{2}
.
Applying Theorem 6.3.2 in
[
53
]
and because

\boldsymbol{g}
is independent of
(\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E},\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})
, we find

{\mathbb{P}}(\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}{/\sigma}\|_{2}\geq\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}\|_{F}+Ct\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{P}_{\boldsymbol{z}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{E})^{-1}\|_{op})\leq 2e^{-t^{2}/2}

for some absolute constant
C>0
.
Combined with (
F.15
) and the union bound,

{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl[\boldsymbol{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{J}\boldsymbol{g}\geq 2\Bigl(\frac{\sigma^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\|_{F}}{(\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-t)_{+}^{2}}+Ct\frac{\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{|J|}+t}{(\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-t)_{+}^{2}}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr]\leq 4e^{-t^{2}/2}.

By concentration of
chi-square distributed random variables with
Tr
degrees of freedom
(e.g., Theorem 5.6 in
[
10
]
), we also have

{\mathbb{P}}(\sigma^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{J}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}\|_{F}\geq\sqrt{T|J|}+t)\leq e^{-t^{2}/2}

since
Tr\leq T|J|
.
Let
s_{*}=\bar{s}+s
and
note that for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\phantom{=}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Bigl\{\Delta\geq\frac{n-T}{\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}}\Bigl[\frac{\sqrt{Ts_{*}}+t+Ct(\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{s_{*}}+t)}{(\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-t)_{+}^{2}}\Bigr]^{2}\Bigr\}\cap\Omega_{*}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset[p]\\
|J|=s_{*}\end{subarray}}\Big\{\Delta\geq\frac{n-T}{\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}}\Bigl[\frac{\sqrt{T|J|}+t+Ct(\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{|J|}+t)}{(\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-t)_{+}^{2}}\Bigr]^{2}\Big\}\cap\{\hat{S}\cup\supp(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\subset J\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset[p]\\
|J|=s_{*}\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big\{\Delta\geq\frac{n-T}{\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}}\Bigl[\frac{\sqrt{T|J|}+t+Ct(\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{|J|}+t)}{(\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-t)_{+}^{2}}\Bigr]^{2}\Big\}\cap\{\hat{S}\cup\supp(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\subset J\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 5\binom{p}{s_{*}}e^{-t^{2}/2},

where the first inequality holds because
|\hat{S}\cup\supp(\boldsymbol{B}^{*})|\leq s_{*}

on
\Omega_{*}
by
Lemma

C.3
.
and the last one is obtained by putting together the previous concentration bounds.
Setting
t=x+(2\log\binom{p}{s_{*}})^{1/2}
,
we find that
\Delta
is smaller than

\frac{n-T}{\sqrt{TF_{T,n-T}}}\Bigl[\frac{\sqrt{Ts_{*}}+\sqrt{2\log\binom{p}{s_{*}}}+x+C(\sqrt{2\log\binom{p}{s_{*}}}+x)(\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{s_{*}}+\sqrt{2\log\binom{p}{s_{*}}}+x)}{(\sqrt{n-1}-\sqrt{T}-\sqrt{2\log\binom{p}{s_{*}}}-x)_{+}^{2}}\Bigr]^{2}

with probability at least
1-5e^{-x^{2}/2}-{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{*}^{c})
.
Since
{\mathbb{E}}[F_{T,n-T}^{-1}]=T/(T-2)
, we have the estimate
F_{T,n-T}^{-1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Under
1.1
(iv) to control the denominator,
and by the bound
\log\binom{p}{s_{*}}\leq s_{*}\log(\tfrac{ep}{s_{*}})

the above display is thus

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\frac{n}{\sqrt{T}}\Bigl[\frac{{Ts}+{s\log(p/s)}+{sT\log(p/s)}+s^{2}\log^{2}(p/s)}{n^{2}}\Bigr]\Bigr)

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\frac{Ts+s\log(p/s)}{n\sqrt{T}}\Bigr)+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\frac{sT\log(p/s)}{n\sqrt{T}}\Bigr)+O_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\frac{s^{2}\log^{2}(p/s)}{n\sqrt{T}}\Bigr).

In the right-hand side, the first term
is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
thanks to
1.1
(iv).
For
n
large enough
\log(p/s)\geq 1
holds, thus the second and third term
are smaller
than
O_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{s(s+T)\log^{2}(p/s)}{n\sqrt{T}})
.
This proves (v).

In order to deduce the upper bound (
F.4
)
from (iii) and (v), it is sufficient to show that

(F.16)

\min\{T/\sqrt{n},s(s+T)\log^{2}(p/s)/(n\sqrt{T})\}=o(1)+o(\log^{2}(p/s)n^{-1/4})

holds under
1.1
.
Let
u_{n}=sT/n
and note that
u_{n}\to 0
by
1.1
.
On the one hand, if
T\leq\max\{\sqrt{n}u_{n},s\}

then
T/\sqrt{n}\leq\max\{u_{n},\sqrt{sT/n}\}=o(1)
.
On the other hand, if
T>\max\{\sqrt{n}u_{n},s\}

then

\frac{s(s+T)\log^{2}(p/s)}{n\sqrt{T}}\leq\frac{2sT\log^{2}(p/s)}{n\sqrt{T}}=\frac{2u_{n}\log^{2}(p/s)}{\sqrt{T}}\leq\frac{2u_{n}^{1/2}\log^{2}(p/s)}{n^{1/4}}=o\Bigl(\frac{\log^{2}(p/s)}{n^{1/4}}\Bigr).

This proves (
F.16
) and completes the proof.
∎

See
4.2

Proof of
Proposition

4.2
.

We first prove case (i).
Then by definition of
q_{T,\alpha}
and the union bound, for any constant
\delta>0

not depending on
n,T
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(W_{n}>q_{T,\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)>\delta)+{\mathbb{P}}((\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}>q_{T,\alpha}-\delta)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)>\delta)+\alpha+{\mathbb{P}}\big((\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}\in[q_{T,\alpha}-\delta,q_{T,\alpha}]\big).

We now bound the third term. Let
f_{T}:[0,+\infty)\to[0,\infty)

be the probability density function of
(\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}
,
which admits the closed form
f_{T}(x)=(2^{T/2-1}\Gamma(T/2))^{-1}x^{T-1}e^{-x^{2}/2}

for
x\geq 0
.
Then

{\mathbb{P}}((\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}\in[q_{T,\alpha}-\delta,q_{T,\alpha}])\leq\delta\sup_{x>0}f_{T}(x)
.
The supremum
\sup_{x>0}f_{T}(x)
is attained at
x=\sqrt{T-1}
, the mode of the chi distribution with
T
degrees of freedom, so that

\textstyle\sup_{x>0}f_{T}(x)=(2^{T/2-1}\Gamma(T/2))^{-1}(T-1)^{(T-1)/2}e^{-(T-1)/2}\xrightarrow[T\to+\infty]{}{1/\sqrt{\pi}}

by Stirling’s formula.
Hence there exists an absolute constant
C_{0}>0
such that

{\mathbb{P}}(W_{n}>q_{T,\alpha})\leq{\mathbb{P}}(o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)>\delta)+\alpha+\delta C_{0}.

For any
\epsilon>0
, let
\delta=\epsilon/C_{0}
.
Using by the definition of convergence
in probability, for
n
large enough we have

{\mathbb{P}}(o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)>\delta)\leq\epsilon
so that

{\mathbb{P}}(W_{n}>q_{T,\alpha})-\alpha\leq 2\epsilon
.
Since this holds for any
\epsilon>0
, the claim is proved.
The same argument can be applied in case (ii) by reversing the inequalities.

∎

Proof of (
4.2
).

The convergence in distribution

(F.17)

\sqrt{2}\bigl((\chi^{2}_{T})^{1/2}-\sqrt{T}\bigr)=\frac{(\sqrt{2T})^{-1}(\chi^{2}_{T}-T)}{(\chi^{2}_{T}/T)^{1/2}/2+1/2}\to^{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

holds by the Central Limit Theorem for

(\sqrt{2T})^{-1}(\chi^{2}_{T}-T)\to^{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,
the weak law of large numbers for

(\chi^{2}_{T}/T)^{1/2}\to^{\mathbb{P}}1

and Slutsky’s theorem. If
\Phi(u)={\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{N}(0,1)\leq u)
is the standard normal cdf,
for any subsequence
(a_{T^{\prime}})_{T^{\prime}}
of

a_{T}=\Phi\bigl(\sqrt{2}(q_{T,\alpha}-\sqrt{T})\bigr)

converging to an accumulation point
L
,
we have for any
\epsilon>0
and
T^{\prime}
large enough

{\mathbb{P}}\bigl[\Phi\bigl(\sqrt{2}((\chi^{2}_{T^{\prime}})^{1/2}-\sqrt{T^{\prime}})\bigr)\leq L-\epsilon\bigr]\leq 1-\alpha\leq{\mathbb{P}}\bigl[\Phi\bigl(\sqrt{2}((\chi^{2}_{T^{\prime}})^{1/2}-\sqrt{T^{\prime}})\bigr)\leq L+\epsilon\bigr]

so that
L-\epsilon\leq 1-\alpha+o(1)
and
1-\alpha\leq L+\epsilon+o(1)

by the weak convergence (
F.17
).
It follows that
L=1-\alpha
is the only accumulation point and

q_{T,\alpha}-\sqrt{T}\to z_{\alpha}/{\sqrt{2}}
, as desired.
∎

G
Proofs for unknown covariance

G.1
Asymptotic normality

Proof of
Theorem

3.3
under assumption (
3.14
).

We will use throughout the proof the notation
defined after (
3.11
)
for
\tau_{j},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}
and
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}
.
Define the direction

{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}=\boldsymbol{e}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1})_{jj}^{-1/2}=\tau_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}

normalized such that
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}\|_{2}=1
by construction,
as well as

{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}=\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}\sim\mathcal{N}_{n}(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{I}_{n})
.
Next, define
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j},{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{T}
by

(G.1)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}+(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j},

(G.2)

\displaystyle{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\left[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\right]\tau_{j}+(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}

so that
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}
coincides with (
4.1
)
for the normalized direction
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}
.
Since the second term in
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}
is the same as the second term in
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}
,

(G.3)

\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}=\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}\Big\{{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\tau_{j}^{-1}\left[n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\right]-{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\Big\}\|_{2}.

Since

\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}=-(\boldsymbol{I}_{p}-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1})_{jj}^{-1}
in (
3.11
),
or equivalently
\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}=\tau_{j}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}
,
we have

{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}=\tau_{j}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}=\tau_{j}^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}).

Next,

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}=\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}=\boldsymbol{X}[\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}]

since by definition of
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}
, the
j
-th coordinate
of
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}
is zero, so that
\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}=\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}
.
By inserting

\boldsymbol{I}_{p\times p}=\sum_{k=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}{}^{\top}

in (
G.3
), using
that the KKT conditions of
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}
imply
that
\max_{k\in[p]}\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\|_{2}\leq nT\lambda

and the triangle inequality, we find

(G.4)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{j}^{-1}\Big\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\Big\{(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)})\left[n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\right]-(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)})\Big\}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau_{j}^{-1}nT\lambda\sum_{k=1}^{p}\Big|\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\Big\{(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)})\left[n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\right]-(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)})\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{j}^{-1}nT\lambda\Big\|\Big\{(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)})\left[n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\right]-(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)})\Big\}\Big\|_{1}.

There are two errors at this point: the estimation error

\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}

and the estimation error
|n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}-1|
,
which corresponds to the relative error of the estimation
of the variance
\tau_{j}^{2}
by
n^{-1}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}

in the linear model (
3.11
).
Keeping these two errors in mind, by the triangle inequality
the previous display yields

(G.5)

\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\tau_{j}^{-1}nT\lambda}{({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})/(n\tau_{j}^{2})}\Big(\Big\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}\Big\|_{1}+\Big\|\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\Big\|_{1}\Big|1-\frac{{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}{n\tau_{j}^{2}}\Big|\Big).

For the first term in the parenthesis, inequality
(
3.13
)
holds: this is the usual
\ell_{1}
estimation rate for
the Lasso estimate
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}
for the sparse
estimation target
\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}
in the linear model
(
3.11
) with
noise variance
\tau_{j}^{2}
.
For the second term,
inequality

(G.6)

\tau_{j}^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}=\tau_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{1}\leq\tau_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{0}^{1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{0}^{1/2}

holds thanks to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
and
\tau_{j}=\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}^{-1}
.
Furthermore, by the triangle inequality, we have

(G.7)

\Big|1-\frac{{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}{n\tau_{j}^{2}}\Big|\leq\Big|1-\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}}{n\tau_{j}^{2}}\Big|+\Big|\frac{({\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}}{n\tau_{j}^{2}}-\frac{{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)})}{n\tau_{j}^{2}}\Big|.

As
\|{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}/\tau_{j}^{2}
has
\chi^{2}_{n}
distribution,
the first term is
O(n^{-1/2})
by the Central Limit Theorem.
For the next term, we use again the triangle inequality.
To bound the next term, notice that by Hölder’s inequality,

|({\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}|=|({\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)})^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}|\leq\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}\|\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}{}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}.

Each factor in the right hand side
is bounded from above as follows:
