\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}=\tau_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}O_{\mathbb{P}}\big(\sqrt{n^{-1}\log p}\big)
thanks to
(
3.13
)
and

\|\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}{}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}=\tau_{j}O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n\log p})

because
\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}
is independent of
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}

and
\max_{k\in[p]\setminus\{j\}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{kk}\leq 1
.
This proves that

|({\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)}|/(n\tau_{j}^{2})\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}\log p)
.
We also have

|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(j)})|=|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}|\leq\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(\tau_{j}\sqrt{n\log p})\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}

thanks to Hölder’s inequality and
the KKT conditions for
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}
in (
3.12
)
to bound the
\ell_{\infty}
norm.
We have

\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}^{1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{2}

and
\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{2}\leq\tau_{j}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}\leq\tau_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\|_{op}

by definition of
\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}

and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Combining these bounds provide an upper bound on the right hand side
of (
G.7
), so that

\displaystyle\Big|1-\frac{{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}}{n\tau_{j}^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)+\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}O_{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{\log p}{n}\Big)+\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\Big(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}\log p}{n}\Big)^{1/2}

(G.8)

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\|_{op}\big({\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}\log(p)}/{n}\big)^{1/2}O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

where the second line follows by bounding from above
the first two terms thanks to assumption
(
3.14
)
and

\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\|_{op}\geq 1
(this is a consequence of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{jj}\leq 1
in
1.1
).
The bound (
G.8
) also provides

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}/(n\tau_{j}^{2})\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

and thus
n\tau_{j}^{2}/({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Using (
3.13
), (
G.6
) and (
G.8
) to bound
from above the right hand side of (
G.5
) we
find

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}\leq nT\lambda\Big(

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n^{-1}\log p})

\displaystyle+\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{0}^{1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}^{1/2}O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n^{-1}\log p})\Big).

Since
\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}=\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{0}-1
,
this implies

\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}\leq nT\lambda\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\|_{op}^{3/2}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{0}O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n^{-1}\log p})
.
Thanks to
\lambda=O\big(\sigma(nT)^{-1/2})(1+\sqrt{\log(p/s)/T})\big)

by definition of
\lambda
, we eventually obtain

(G.9)

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\big([\sqrt{T}+\sqrt{\log(p/s)}]\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{0}\sqrt{\log(p)/n}\big)

which converges to 0 in probability
thanks to assumption (
3.14
).

To complete the proof of
Theorem

3.3
and prove asymptotic normality
for some fixed
\boldsymbol{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{T}
with
\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}=1
, notice that

\zeta_{j}\coloneqq\frac{n{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}^{\top}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\boldsymbol{b}+{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}}{\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}}

satisfies
\zeta_{j}\smash{\xrightarrow{d}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
by
Theorem

3.1

applied to the normalized direction
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}
.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\Big|\zeta_{j}-\frac{n\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\boldsymbol{b}+n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}}{(\tau_{j})^{-1}~\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle=\frac{|(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}|}{\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\|_{op}\Big(\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}^{-1}(\sigma^{2}n)^{1/2}\Big).

In the above display,

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}0

when (
3.14
) holds,

\|(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\|_{op}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

by
Proposition

2.1
(iii) and
Lemma

C.3
,
and the rightmost factor converges to 1 in probability
by
Theorem

3.2
.

Since
\tau_{j}=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1})_{jj}^{-1/2}
,
the last claim follows by

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}/(n\tau_{j}^{2})\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

by (
G.8
) and Slutsky’s theorem.
We also have

\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}/(\tau_{j}\sqrt{n})\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1
since,
using (
3.12
) and the triangle inequality,

(G.10)

\displaystyle(\tau_{j}^{2}n)^{-1}|\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}|

\displaystyle=(\tau_{j}^{2}n)^{-1}|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}|

(G.11)

\displaystyle\leq(\tau_{j}^{2}n)^{-1}O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\tau_{j}\sqrt{n\log p}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}\|_{1}

(G.12)

\displaystyle\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\sqrt{\log(p)/n}\big[\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{1}\big]/\tau_{j}

(G.13)

\displaystyle\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\sqrt{\log(p)/n}\big[\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}\sqrt{\log(p)/n}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}^{1/2}\big]

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

thanks to (
3.13
) for the first term
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for the second.
The convergence to 0 in probability in the last line follows from
(
3.14
).
∎

Proof of
Theorem

3.3
under assumption (
3.15
).

With
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}
in (
G.2
)
and
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}]\tau_{j}

we have

\displaystyle\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})^{\top}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}+\tau_{j}(\boldsymbol{B}^{*}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}]\|_{2}

(G.14)

\displaystyle\leq\|\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{op}+\tau_{j}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{2,1}\|\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{\infty}|[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})]^{-1}|

thanks to
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1/2}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}\|_{2}=1
for the first term
and
Hölder’s inequality for the second
term.
Thanks to
Lemma

C.2
(iii),
Lemma

C.3
and
Proposition

2.1
we find

\|\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\|_{op}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\|_{op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{s}\bar{R})
.
For the second term, thanks to (
3.12
)
and
Lemma

C.2
(iv) we have

\tau_{j}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\|_{2,1}\|\boldsymbol{X}_{-j}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{\infty}|[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})]^{-1}|\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{s}\bar{R})\sqrt{n\log p}\big|[n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})]^{-1}\big|

and the bound (
G.8
) grants

{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}/(n\tau_{j}^{2})\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1
thanks to the leftmost assumption
in (
3.15
).
In summary,

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}s\bar{R}+\sqrt{s}\bar{R}\sqrt{\log p})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{s}\bar{R}\sqrt{\log p})

thanks to
n^{-1/2}\sqrt{s}\leq 1
.
Hence due to the rightmost assumption
in (
3.15
),

(G.15)

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}0.

Next, assume without loss of generality that
\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}=1
.
By definition of
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}
in (
G.2
),

(G.16)

\displaystyle\frac{n\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\boldsymbol{b}+n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}}{(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1})_{jj}^{1/2}~\sigma\sqrt{n}}-\frac{{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}

(G.17)

\displaystyle=\frac{({\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}-\frac{{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1})\boldsymbol{b}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}.

The first term converges to
0
in probability thanks to
the previous paragraph, while the second
term is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(s/n)\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}

by
Proposition

2.1
and
Lemma

C.3
.

\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{j}\coloneqq[n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}]^{-1}\tau_{j}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}^{-1}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}
has
\mathcal{N}_{T}(\mathbf{0},\sigma^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T})

distribution
by independence of
\boldsymbol{E}
and
\boldsymbol{X}
.
Next,

\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}=\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}[n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}]\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{j}\|_{2}

and
\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{j}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{T})
since
{\mathbb{E}}[\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}]=T
.
Furthermore,

n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

by (
G.8
).
We also have

\tau_{j}\sqrt{n}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}^{-1}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1
by
(
G.13
),
thanks to the leftmost assumption in (
3.15
) for the last line in (
G.13
).
This shows that
\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\|_{2}/(\sqrt{n}\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{j}\|_{2})\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

and that the second term in (
G.17
)
is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(s/n)
and converges to
0
in probability.
We conclude by observing that
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}/(\sigma\sqrt{n})\smash{\xrightarrow{d}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

by Slutsky’s theorem
thanks to

n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

and

\tau_{j}\sqrt{n}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}^{-1}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1
.
In the denominator,

\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}

and
\sigma\sqrt{n}
can be used interchangeably,
again by Slutsky’s theorem,
since

\|(\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{X}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T\times T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}/(\sigma\sqrt{n})\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

by
Theorem

3.2
.
∎

G.2
Asymptotic
\chi^{2}_{T}
distribution

Proof of
Theorem

4.3
under
assumption (
3.15
)
.

Let
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}
and
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}
be defined respectively
in (
G.2
) and in the sentence preceding
(
G.14
).
Notice that the quantity in the left hand side of (
4.7
) is equal to

(1-T/n)^{1/2}\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}{}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{2}

where

(G.18)

\boldsymbol{\xi}=\bigl(\tau_{j}^{-1}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\bigr]^{-1}\bigr){\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}.

Set
\boldsymbol{z}={\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}/\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}
.
For these values of
\boldsymbol{\xi}
and
\boldsymbol{z}
,
we have

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\Big\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\tau_{j}^{-1}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\Bigr]^{-1}-\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\Big\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\Big\|_{2}\tau_{j}^{-1}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}]^{-1}.

Hence the above is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by combining
(
G.15
)
with (
G.8
) and (
G.13
).
An application of
Lemma

F.3

for these values of
\boldsymbol{z}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}

yields
(
F.4
)
which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

4.3
under
assumption (
3.14
)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}
be defined in (
G.1
)
Since
{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j},{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}
defined in the proof of

Theorem

3.3
satisfy
the assumptions of
Theorem

4.1
, we have already
established that

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}-\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}^{-1}\|_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, cf. (
F.1
)
with
\boldsymbol{a}={\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{j}
and
\boldsymbol{z}_{0}={\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}
.

We now proceed to show that

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\|_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

for
\boldsymbol{\xi}
defined in (
G.18
).
By the triangle inequality
and since
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}
in (
G.18
)
is proportional to
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}
, we have

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\Big\|_{2}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}\frac{\tau_{j}^{-1}\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\Bigr]^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\Big\|({\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j})\frac{\tau_{j}^{-1}\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\Bigr]^{-1}+\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\Bigl(\frac{\tau_{j}^{-1}\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\Bigr]^{-1}-1\Bigr)\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\Big\|({\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j})\Big\|_{2}\frac{\tau_{j}^{-1}\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\Bigr]^{-1}+\Big\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\Big\|_{2}\Bigl|\frac{\tau_{j}^{-1}\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\Bigr]^{-1}-1\Bigr|.

For the first term, by (
G.9
)
we already have

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\|_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Combined
with
\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}/(\tau_{j}\sqrt{n})\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

and

n\tau_{j}^{2}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\smash{\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{P}}}}1

(see (
G.8
) and (
G.13
)),
this proves that the first term above is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

For the remaining terms,

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{T})

by (
F.1
),
and the question is whether

(G.19)

O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{T})\Big|\frac{\tau_{j}^{-1}\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\Bigr]^{-1}-1\Big|

converges to 0 using (
G.8
)
and (
G.13
).
With
a_{j}=\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}/(\tau_{j}^{2}n)

and
b_{j}={\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}/(\tau_{j}^{2}n)
for brevity,

\displaystyle\big|\tau_{j}^{-1}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}}\Bigl[n({\widehat{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{-1}\Bigr]^{-1}-1\big|

\displaystyle=a_{j}^{-1/2}|b_{j}-a^{1/2}|

\displaystyle\leq a_{j}^{-1/2}(|b_{j}-1|+|1-a_{j}^{1/2}|)

\displaystyle=a_{j}^{-1/2}(|b_{j}-1|+|1-a_{j}|(1+a_{j})^{-1}).

We have

|a_{j}-1|+|b_{j}-1|=\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(j)}\|_{0}\log(p)/n}~O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

thanks to (
G.8
) and (
G.13
).
Hence thanks to (
3.14
),
quantity (
G.19
) is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Combining all the pieces, we have proved that

(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\sqrt{n}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}^{-1}\|_{2}\leq(\sigma^{2}n)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\xi}_{j}\|_{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\leq o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Applying

Lemma

F.3
to
\boldsymbol{\xi}
in (
G.18
)
and
\boldsymbol{z}={\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{z}}}_{j}\|_{2}^{-1}
,
conclusion
(
F.4
) completes the proof.
∎
